It is just a question of gradually waking up and realizing that you have actually brought us to the spiritual world.

Therefore please continue to accept our attempts to serve you. And to be able to serve you in the best way, we pray that you please give us the association of those followers of yours who know your heart and thus, by their example and realization, can show what it means to be a Prabhupādānuga.

Your aspiring servants in ISKCON Stockholm, Sweden.

## Sydney

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,I offer my humble and prostrated obeisances at your lotus feet.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

The year 2011 marks the fortieth anniversary of your arrival on the shores of this big southern land. In May of this year we celebrated the milestone event with great festivity and joy. More than 150 of your initiated Australian disciples converged on your ISKCON Sydney temple and joined with hundreds of second- and third-generation disciples to glorify the day in 1971 when you installed the Divine Couple Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Gopīnātha in Their tiny storefront temple here and entrusted Their care into our unqualified hands.

We recalled how unsuitable we were to look after Them, and how you requested Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa to empower us and inspire us to come up to the proper standard.

When you returned into our midst almost a year later and saw with joy the sparkling new temple and altar in the Sydney suburb of Glebe, you told us, “When I last left, I prayed to Lord Kṛṣṇa: ‘You please give help to these boys and girls, all *mlecchas*, to worship you nicely.’ Now I am very happy to see that He has given you such good intelligence. Everything is very nice.” At that time perhaps hardly one of us could recall the moment only ten months earlier when you had stood before Rādhā-Gopīnātha with folded hands, and even if we had, we could not have known the details of your intimate prayer.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you were fond of quoting this half-verse from *Caitanya-caritāmṛta:* *vaiṣṇavera kriyā, mudrā vijñeha nā bujhaya*. “Even the most intelligent person of this world cannot understand the activities of a Vaiṣṇava.”

But the fact remained that on your inaugural visit you had indeed stated to your worshipable Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa that since you were leaving They would have to empower us, his *mleccha* caretakers, to look after Them. You had in fact madd this extraordinary request:

Now I am leaving You in the hands of the *mlecchas*. I cannot take the responsibility. You please guide these boys and girls and give them the intelligence to worship You nicely.

*Mleccha* is a term used to denote those meat-eating men and women who do not conform to the moral and religious norms of the Vedic society. You write in your purport to *Śrīmad*-*Bhāgavatam* 1.16.20: “The people of this age will not perform any sacrifice. The *mleccha* population will care very little for performances of sacrifices. ”

Yet you had entrusted us with the intricacies of Deity worship. How was that possible?

Elsewhere in *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (2.4.18) Śukadeva *Gosvāmī* recites a famous verse (a favorite of mine) that you often quoted in your lectures and books:

*kirāta-hūṇāndhra-pulinda-pulkaśā*  
*ābhīra-śumbhā yavanāḥ khasādayaḥ*  
*ye ’nye ca pāpā yad-apāśrayāśrayāḥ*  
*śudhyanti tasmai prabhaviṣṇave namaḥ*

**“Kirātas, Hūṇas, Āndhras, Pulindas, Pulkaśas, Ābhīras, Śumbhas, Yavanas, members of the Khasa races, and even others addicted to sinful acts can be purified by taking shelter of devotees of the Lord, for He is the supreme power. I beg to offer my respectful obeisances unto Him.”**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, during your first visit you gave me spiritual initiation, and some of us you also gave second initiation, and along with it the authority to worship the Deity in the temple. So our *mleccha* habits would hopefully be a thing of the past. You were also fond of quoting a verse from the *Tattva-sāgara* that Śrīla Sanātana *Gosvāmī* includes in his *Hari-bhakti-vilāsa* (2.12):

*yathā kāñcanatāṁ yāti kāṁsyaṁ rasa-vidhānataḥ*  
*tathā dīkṣā-vidhānena dvijatvaṁ jāyate nṛṇām*

**“As bell metal can turn to gold when mixed with mercury in a chemical process, so, by bona fide** ***dīkṣā,*** **or initiation, anyone can become a Vaiṣṇava.”**

Lowborn boys and girls like ourselves had been given the opportunity to become *brāhmaṇas*. Such is your power, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Your entrusting worshipable Deities into the hands of ex-*mlecchas* did not go unnoticed in the Vaiṣṇava world. You later recalled in Melbourne:

The others are seeing “How these *mlecchas* and *yavanas* can become Vaiṣṇavas?” They are seeing like that. Therefore they object. In Vṛndāvana they protest: “How can these Europeans worship the Deity?” The point is that when one becomes qualified, he is no longer a demon.

It was in this spirit that you continued to expand your mission in what you referred to as “the southernmost part of this globe” by installing Deities on your subsequent six visits to Australia and New Zealand. Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Giridhārī appeared in Auckland, and Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa, Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Vallabha, and Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi appeared in Melbourne, and they were all personally installed by your loving hands.

And although you did not personally install the ever-smiling forms of Jagannātha, Baladeva, and Subhadrā in Melbourne, your disciples Dīpaka Dāsa, Nartakī Devī Dāsī, and Dvaipāyana Dāsa had carved them lovingly before their inaugural appearance at Ratha-yātrā in 1972. In fact, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you were particularly enthusiastic to establish the worship of Jagannātha in the West. As you explained in a letter to a friend in India:

When we first start a temple, we start with Jagannatha Swami. My guru maharaja recommended temples of Jagannatha in these countries because He is kind even to the mlecchas.

You visited Sydney for the last time at the quaint house-cum-temple in Hereford Street, Glebe, in 1973, and again you spoke encouragingly about how impressed you were with the gorgeous worship of Rādhā-Gopīnātha. In fact, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you recalled the wonderful pastimes of Rādhā-Gopīnātha on a number of occasions in your remaining years with us in this world. You were speaking in your room at the home of Ugraśravā Dāsa in Melbourne on Tuesday, 2 July 1974:

Śrīla Prabhupāda: Hmm. Nice pictures. In Sydney, the Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa Deity is also nice, very nice. What is this?

Dīpaka: These are our Tulasī plants, Śrīla Prabhupāda. They grow very nicely.

Prabhupāda: Hmm. There’s also very nice worship at Sydney. Eh?

Dīpaka: Those pictures were taken on Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s appearance day.

Prabhupāda: Oh? Kṛṣṇa is giving intelligence. The more you serve Kṛṣṇa, the more He will give you intelligence. \[You look at the photographs of Rādhā-Gopīnātha and recall your first visit to Sydney in 1971.\]

So, when I installed the Deity, then I prayed to Kṛṣṇa, that “I am leaving You in their hands, so give them intelligence and take Your service. No responsibility. Now it is Your business. Give them intelligence and they will serve You nicely. Otherwise, don’t take my offense.” \[The devotees laugh affectionately.\]

Yes, when I left Sydney after installing, I actually prayed to the Deity: “I am leaving You here in this *mleccha-deśa*. \[More laughter.\] Now, these devotees, they have come to You. Kindly give them intelligence so that they can serve You nicely. This is my prayer.”

Cāru: You said we were all nonsense at that time.

Prabhupāda: \[Agreeing with Cāru, you nod and add:\] But Kṛṣṇa will give you sense.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, on this auspicious day of your appearance, we devotees in the Sydney temple thank you for requesting Kṛṣṇa to give us good sense. If we remain pure and never return to our *mleccha* ways, then surely you will continue to shower us with your mercy and empower us to make advancement on the path of pure devotional service, the goal of the lives we have dedicated to you.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

The devotees at the Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Gopīnātha Temple in Sydney, Australia.  
(written by Kūrma Dāsa)

## Taiwan

Our dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to you!

Even though many of us never saw you in person in this lifetime, we are convinced there is a close bond between us. You are so familiar to us. Whenever we study your books, we deeply feel your personal presence in our heart of hearts, beyond the limits of time and space. Your profound, straightforward, wise words have guided us through lots of hardships and trials since we met you in your teachings. The more we fulfill your desire by spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness to the people of Taiwan, the more we develop faith in you and love for you.

It’s your teachings that make us understand that our real happiness comes from cooperating with the Lord and serving Him in a loving relationship.

It’s you that make us realize the infinite power and bliss resulting from the nectar of the *saṅkīrtana* movement.

It’s you that make us experience the spiritual sweetness and attraction of purity and humility through your advanced disciples all over the world.

It’s your mercy that provides us with a real home for self-realization with good association.

It’s you that help us distinguish temporary, invalid material opulence from the true value of the eternal blissful inner world connected with Kṛṣṇa.

It’s you that help us uproot the false belief that we are the doer and the enjoyer of the results of our actions.

The more we preach, the more we realize how tough it is to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness in the material world in this Kali-yuga, when people are so much influenced by sinful activities and where lust, anger, envy, and greed prevail.

How brave a spiritual warrior you were to fight and save people from the endless, miserable cycle of birth and death in the barren wasteland of the material world!

On this auspicious day of your Vyāsa-pūjā, we meditate on one of the wonderful prayers you composed in September 1965, just after you arrived in Boston carrying within your heart the orders of your spiritual master to spread the teachings of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu throughout the entire world.

Only by Your causeless mercy will my words become pure. I’m sure that when this transcendental message penetrates their hearts they will certainly feel engladdened and thus become liberated from all unhappy conditions of life.

These ever-fresh and beautiful prayers to the Lord you wrote upon your arrival in the West have very much touched us, for they perfectly express your compassionate mood as a humble servant of the Lord. These prayers also remind us of our real identity as a servant of Kṛṣṇa and protect us from the deep-rooted selfish desire for recognition and adoration, especially when preaching.

We pray for your blessings, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Please bestow upon us determination and a fearless spirit, so we may conquer our weaknesses and continue rendering our service in your mission under your protection, free from the influence of false ego.

All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Your humble servants in Taiwan.  
(written by Śyāmasundarī Devī Dāsī)

## Togo

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our prostrated obeisances at the dust of your lotus feet.

The time of year has arrived when we solemnly come before you to render an account of our progress in Kṛṣṇa consciousness and to report on what we have been doing with the talents you have given to us through the various aspects of Vedic culture.

You taught us how to arm ourselves sufficiently, with your teachings, for our protection against *māyā,* and to be able to equally protect others also. You said you were especially glad when you noted that all your disciples were feeling so much enthusiasm to work very hard to spread this preaching mission. That enthusiasm must be maintained under all circumstances. That is our price for entering Kṛṣṇa’s kingdom. And *māyā* is always trying to take away our enthusiasm to serve Kṛṣṇa, because without enthusiasm everything else is finished. This is one of your lessons to us.

Sometimes we are proud of how much service we have done, or how elevated we have become, but the truth is that we are being monitored by Kṛṣṇa and His agents. Our elevation in consciousness has been arranged by them for a higher purpose. As long as that purpose is served, we remain rock-bed solid in our position, but as soon as that purpose—Kṛṣṇa’s plan—is not served by our so-called success or advancement, then everything falls apart like a collapsing house of cards.

I am short of words to explain how abundantly your mercy has been coming through, especially at the most difficult times, and how the Lord’s amazing grace has seen us through our darkest nights. At the end of each encounter we rise and give praise to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for your intercession on our behalf, for your supplication to the Lord at our time of need.

We pray that with each passing year we can still be here to say “Thank you” for saving us. We pray that through the many years to come our many dangers, toils, and snares will simply provide subject matter for us to recount the blissful experience of being protected and kept safe in all circumstances by your mercy, and ultimate we pray that you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, will lead us back home, back to Godhead.

Your servants Lome, Togo.  
(written by Varāha Dāsa)

## Trinidad

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble, respectful obeisances at the dust of your divine lotus feet.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I feel very privileged and honored to have been asked by the devotees of our temples here in Trinidad to write this offering to Your Divine Grace. I also feel very unqualified to do so, since I have no proper intelligence to chose the proper words to glorify such a great and pure devotee of the Supreme Lord as Your Divine Grace. I can therefore only pray to Lord Kṛṣṇa to please give me the words so that I can make this offering of some worth.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, words are too limited and time is insufficient to describe all your qualities. It would not be possible for us, even if we had countless mouths like Lord Ananta or the lifetime of Lord *Brahmā*. Still, we pray that you will accept this heartfelt though meager offering.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, on the order of your Guru Mahārāja you came to the Western world to teach us what the genuine higher taste is, and how anyone can experience it through the wonderful process of devotional service to the Supreme Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa. You built a house, this great ISKCON movement, that can shelter all of mankind. You gave us your books and the holy names of God, and you also taught us by your own example how to serve the Supreme Lord. All of this so that we might become qualified to go home, back to Godhead.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, on behalf of all the devotees of the Trinidad *yātrā,* I would like to say thank you for all the special mercy you have shown us.

Your disciples and granddisciples in Trinidad.  
(written by Rasika Mauli Dāsa)

## Villaggio Hare Krishna

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

The year 2000 marked the beginning of the Śrīla Prabhupāda Millennium. This is the first century that began with your glories already known to the world. It will end with your personality, teachings, and achievements more clearly understood by the human inhabitants of this planet. We, the members of your ISKCON society, have for the last forty-five years been attempting to educate the world about the fantastic achievements of your lifetime, thus helping to perpetuate the standards of religious living you kindly brought to our attention. You were fully convinced that the Hare Kṛṣṇa movement would save a humanity “gone south,” so to speak, and you considered it the only panacea for the crippled cultures of this planet. You took it upon yourself to help folks who view declining moral principles as a form of advancement and who perform degraded activities while leading pointless lives in pursuit of material gratification—or, in other words, just about everyone.

Who would even take the time to bother with such a despicable lot as our wretched Western selves? The powerful leaders, big politicians, had a solution for us: lies. They fed us more and more promises that through science and technology we would soon reach the end of our incompleteness and finally reach fulfillment, realizing full happiness. Maybe we would find it on the moon!

But you took the time, Śrīla Prabhupāda, to teach us the real path to fulfillment. Unselfishly, you considered the human race worth saving. Displaying the love and compassion that only a kind parent can feel, you took mankind under your wing. With understanding that no one else of this era could manifest, you surrendered your total energy to enlightening dying humanity, and blessed them with real transcendental knowledge. Certainly this describes not the stuff of ordinary dealings, or the character of any ordinary man, but the activities of a true modern saint—very rare.

I lived through the last part of the previous millennium, witnessed the ebbing fortunes of so many disappointed souls (including myself), who had harbored hopes for happiness and success but had gotten on the wrong path, boarded the wrong train. In the early 60s I and many other disciples of yours went through adolescence—puberty, high school, and all the rest. *Something* was happening; everyone seemed to sense it. Change was in the air. Suddenly, rock & roll bands were all singing about it, young people were sporting long hair and ragged dress and trying to “get high” through drugs and intoxication. Space exploration, assassinations, political upheavals, questioning of values, spiritual searching—all became part of the norm.

We know now what was *really* happening. You, Śrīla Prabhupāda, were introducing a strange new serum into society’s veins. It was the “drug” of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, the Hare Kṛṣṇa mantra. Your timing, Kṛṣṇa’s timing, couldn’t have been better—just what the doctor ordered!

The way you changed things was phenomenal. In an amazingly short time, through your fortitude, you accomplished what your godbrothers and your family and friends had thought impossible. Suddenly, in one decade, there were temples all over the globe, major festivals, millions of books published and distributed, vegetarian cooking classes, *gurukulas* established, etc. This was totally unprecedented. But you pulled it off, in your humble, unassuming way, as though introducing God’s holy names into the midst of an ignorant, demoniac culture was the most natural thing to do! Well, for you it *was* natural, since you never compromised your pure spiritual principles.

As you said in the last stanza of the poem to your spiritual master, Śrīla *Bhaktisiddhānta* Sarasvatī:

The oldest of all,  
But in new dress.  
Miracle done,  
Your Divine Grace

We can appreciate that you would want to give all credit to your spiritual master, and that he would credit his spiritual master, who in turn would credit his, all the way back to Lord Caitanya and ultimately Kṛṣṇa Himself. Such is the way of transcendental spiritual transmission: we develop so much appreciation and love for our immediate (and predecessor) benefactors.

You, Śrīla Prabhupāda, being Kṛṣṇa’s pure representative and instrument, are the holy beacon of the modern age, a saint whose glorious activities many of us were fortunate enough to personally witness. So it is in fact an understatement to say that we Kali-yuga spirit sparks are forever indebted to you, and that we should thank you eternally.

Your servants at Villaggio Hare Krishna, Italy.  
(written by Rudradeva Dāsa)

## Vṛndāvana

To our dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

All of the devotees and pilgrims here at your wonderful Śrī Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma Mandira fall down at your lotus feet hundreds of times in prostrated obeisances. We are moved to thank you every morning in *guru*-*pūjā* and at every *kīrtana* for establishing this spiritual home for the many aspiring devotees from around the world who have become attracted to Vṛndāvana due to your preaching and your books. By your grace, millions of conditioned souls are now receiving the blessings and mercy of the holy names of the Lord.

Until you traveled outside of Śrī Vṛndāvana *dhāma*, with no money or support, at the age of seventy and in very poor health, no one had successfully taken the mission and message of *Śrī* *Gaurāṅga Mahāprabhu* outside India. After going to the West, with only your infallible *bhakti* to attract us, you converted thousands of *mlecchas* to Kṛṣṇa consciousness. After returning back to the soft sands of Ramaṇa Reti as a victorious hero, you built Śrī Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma Mandira, which is now the most popular temple in all of Vṛndāvana, and for that matter the entire world.

We are focused on distributing your books and distributing *prasādam,* but our number-one priority is the perpetual chanting of the *mahā-mantra*. The *kīrtana* is always going on in the temple, and millions of pilgrims are amazed at how the powerful transcendental sound vibration is moving them and transforming their lives.

Your Bhaktivedanta Gurukula is doing very well indeed, with good leadership, more students than ever, and qualified teachers who are training the students to regularly enter the temple for chanting and *darśana*. With your blessings and by your desire, we hope to develop the project according to your directions.

In your last months, in Vṛndāvana, you expressed your desire to establish *varṇāśrama* based cow protection and living off the land. Until now we have not made much progress toward this divine goal, but now we are praying to your lotus feet to give us the intelligence to understand how to manifest this socially revolutionary concept in this wretched age of *ugra-karma*. Perhaps the devotees of your Śrī Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma Mandira can help lead the world in *go-sevā* and living off the land. Then your mission of preaching Kṛṣṇa consciousness and establishing *daivī*-*varṇāśrama* could both be said to have begun here in Śrī Vṛndāvana *dhāma* and then been spread all over the world, bringing about a full re-spiritualization of human society.

We all thank you for everything you have given us: a new life of freedom, happiness, and Vaiṣṇava *saṅga,* and the opportunity to render devotional service to the Supreme Lord, which illuminates the path back home, back to Godhead.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I and all the kind devotees at Śrī Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma Mandira simply desire to become fully surrendered to you and to enthusiastically engage in your service.

We beg to always remain under the shelter of your lotus feet.

Your devotees at the Śrī Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma Mandira, Śrī Vṛndāvana dhāma.  
(written by Pañcagauḍa Dāsa)

## Washington, D.C.

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your divine lotus feet.

Today is the most auspicious day of your appearance. On this day the devotees of New Hastināpura *dhāma,* for their own purification, request your permission to glorify you, reminisce about you, and report to you the current state of affairs.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, in February 1936, in Bombay, hundreds of Vaiṣṇavas listened spellbound to your powerful and eloquent offering to your *gurudeva,* Śrīla *Bhaktisiddhānta* Sarasvatī *Gosvāmī*. You stated:

But all of us can lend our eager ears for the aural reception of the transcendental sound transmitted from that region to this through the unadulterated medium of Śrī Gurudeva or Śrī Vyāsadeva. Therefore, gentlemen, we should surrender ourselves today at the feet of the representative of Śrī Vyāsadeva for the elimination of all our differences bred by our unsubmissive attitude.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, as you spoke, so you practiced—you are an *ācārya* in the truest sense of the word. You were so eager to hear from your Guru Mahārāja that he noticed and remarked, “This boy does not go away. He hears.” In a conversation about your Guru Mahārāja, you once mentioned with characteristic humility, “In the beginning I could not understand what he was speaking, but I wanted to hear him. That’s all. I was very much anxious to hear him. That he marked. And he was kindly pleased upon me.”

You not only heard faithfully but also absorbed what you heard and followed his instructions. In November 1935, standing by Rādhā-kuṇḍa, he told you, “If you ever get money, print books.” And you certainly printed books, Śrīla Prabhupāda. You produced more than sixty volumes of commentated English translations of classic Vedic scriptures. For their authority, depth, and clarity, your books have won praise from professors at colleges and universities like Harvard, Oxford, Cornell, Columbia, Syracuse, Oberlin, and Edinburgh. Your *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is* is now available in over sixty languages around the world. Every year millions of people receive and read your books and learn about Kṛṣṇa.

In 1922, at your first meeting with your future spiritual master, he immediately remarked to you and your companion, “You are educated young men. Why don’t you preach Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s message throughout the entire world?” You took the seed of this instruction in your heart and carefully nurtured it for over four decades. After a thirty-five-day sea journey from Calcutta, you reached Boston’s Commonwealth Pier at 5:30 a.m. on September 17, 1965, with just a few books, a bag of dry cereal, and forty rupees. But with unflinching faith in the order of Śrī Guru, you inundated the world with the mercy of Caitanya Mahāprabhu. The echoes of *harināma* you initiated are still reverberating in the hearts of your disciples and granddisciples. Śrīla Prabhupāda, appreciation of the gravity of your movement is now becoming more and more widespread. On January 28, 2011, Wikipedia featured on its front page, as the “Picture of the Day,” the poster advertising the “Mantra-Rock Dance,” an event held exactly forty-four years earlier in which you chanted the Hare Kṛṣṇa mantra at the Avalon Ballroom in San Francisco. The poster showed an image of you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, with links to articles on you, the Hare Kṛṣṇa mantra, and ISKCON. More than five million people saw this picture, got your *darśana,* and read the mantra. Recently you were also profiled in the CNN’s Living section, entitled “Famous Folks Who Launched Careers After 50.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have mercifully entrusted us with a wonderful legacy. We are grateful that you have given us some service for our own purification. Here at New Hastināpura *dhāma* we carefully hold your instructions in our heart and try to manifest them in our actions. Even though you were equally merciful to everyone, we consider ourselves to be special to you. On March 1, 1976, when we established the temple in its current location in Potomac, Maryland, you were so pleased that despite your ill health and other commitments, you quickly came to visit us in July 1976. In the course of your week’s stay with us, you shared your vision and gave us many valuable instructions. While you were giving a class, a devotee asked you about how to deal with skepticism. You answered emphatically, “Skepticism is rascalisim and we do not deal with rascals!” Just as you were *vyavasāyātmikā* (single-minded) in following the instructions of your *gurudeva,* you wanted us to be the same.

When the Rādhā-Dāmodara Deities meant for our temple decided instead to travel with the *saṅkīrtana* party, you very mercifully gave us your personal deity, *Śrī* Madana-Mohana. He is the oldest Deity in ISKCON, personally worshiped by you since 1967. We are situated in one of the most passionate cities of one of the most materialistic countries in the world. It is very easy for us to forget who we are and how we are related to the Supreme Personality. The special mercy of Madana-Mohana is *sambandha*-*jñāna,* knowledge that reminds us of our relationship with Kṛṣṇa. By giving us the service of Madana-Mohana, you wanted us to carefully develop and be situated in this understanding of our relationship with Him and with you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

When you visited us, you went to see the Fourth of July Independence Day Parade in Washington, D.C. Today this parade serves as a prelude to our Ratha-yātrā. A colossal chariot bearing Lord Jagannātha, Lord Balarāma, and Śrīmatī Subhadrā rolls down the Washington Mall amid a sea of dancing devotees chanting the Hare Kṛṣṇa mantra. From the Capitol building the parade goes about a mile to the spectacular festival grounds at the Washington Monument. Thousands of people get direct *darśana,* and millions watch the Deities on television. We also organize the Festival of India on this day. More than ten thousand plates of *prasādam* are distributed, devotees perform ecstatic *kīrtanas* and plays, and hundreds of your books are distributed. The nectar of the holy name gushes from the grounds of the US Capitol and engulfs the thousands of people who come for the festivities.

Our festivals at the temple are becoming larger and grander. For Kṛṣṇa-janmāṣṭamī more than five thousand people come to take *darśana* of the Deities, honor *prasādam,* and chant the holy name. Understanding your desire that capital cities be ruled by the perfect ruler, Lord *Rāma*, in 1982 we became the first temple in the United States to install Lord Rāmacandra, His consort *Sītā*, His brother Lakṣmaṇa, and His devotee *Hanumān*. Our annual Dussehra festivities, which end with the burning of a twenty-five-foot-tall effigy of Rāvaṇa, is the talk of the town and attracts more than four thousand visitors. In addition to our weekly Sunday Love Feasts, we organize fourteen major festivals every year. The temple has become an integral part of the lives of many families who accept initiation, take marriage vows, do the grain ceremony for their children, and perform many other *saṁskāras* under the auspicious glance of our Deities.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you were always eager to look for new ways to expand Kṛṣṇa consciousness. In the same mood, we have been increasingly using the new Internet technologies to connect people with our temple and Kṛṣṇa. As part of our outreach program, we organize several Bhakti-vṛksas to allow devotees to associate with peers and develop long-term loving relationships. We have also increased our college preaching and book distribution. Through regular interfaith dialogs we are gaining the respect and appreciation of leading members of other religions and educating them about ours. We are also developing close relationships with other Hindu temples in the area. Recognizing that children need to be guided from an early age, we have an active Sunday school program that teaches them the basic tenets of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. As we write this, we are in the process of expanding the classroom facilities to accommodate the increased participation. In our hearts we nourish your desire that one day this temple will become so attractive that it will be visited by US Senators and their wives. We have begun planning for a new temple building. It is a large project requiring much endeavor, and we pray for your guidance, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

While our material vision is contaminated and limited, your transcendental vision is not. Once while sitting on a park bench in New York, you described to Mr. Ruben, a friendly subway-toll collector, your actual wealth—temples, books, and devotees. This was 1966, just a few months after you had arrived to the U.S., and you were factually all alone. Yet you looked down the passage of time and revealed to this fortunate person what you saw. While waiting on the *Jaladuta* in Boston you compiled the famous *Mārkine Bhāgavata-dharma* prayers. Once again employing your transcendental vision, you prayed to Kṛṣṇa for us: “I wish that You may deliver them. Therefore if You so desire their deliverance, then only will they be able to understand Your message.” By your prayers, Śrīla Prabhupāda, unqualified souls like us have become eligible to obtain the personal association of Kṛṣṇa. You are so kind that you not only showed us the destination and the path, but you demonstrated by personally walking the path with us and removing all the obstacles. All we need to do is to follow you.

While conversing with your disciples here in Potomac, you spoke with gratitude toward your disciples: “I take America as my fatherland. India is motherland, and here is fatherland.” You spoke with fondness: “So many fathers. My father was very affectionate to me. He would do everything for me. I lost one father, but now I have so many fathers.” Such humility can be manifest only in the heart of a pure devotee. It is you who are our father, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Without you we would have no life to speak of, and yet you are gratified by even the smallest service. Please bless us that we may be able to imbibe that mood of gratitude and appreciation toward you, Kṛṣṇa, the devotees, and indeed everyone.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, it is customary to give a person gifts on his appearance day. But instead, on this day we shamelessly ask something from you: Please help us. You would say we should plan to make this our last birth in the material world. Please ignite in us the sense of urgency to go back home, back to Godhead. You frequently said that Kṛṣṇa is for everyone. Please empower us to spread His glories to people of every race, creed, and nationality. The basis of preaching is practice—*ācār* before *prācār*. By your mercy may the conviction of a strong and steady *sādhana* become deeply rooted in our hearts and manifest in our actions. You repeatedly told us that everything we need to know is there in your books. Please protect us from deviant philosophies and give us the intellectual conviction to systematically read your books with faith. Just as you were uncompromising in your message of *śuddha-bhakti,* may we also be able to guide the souls we come in contact with from the path of fruitive devotion to that of unalloyed devotion. Our hearts long to once again hear the roar of both the *mṛdaṅgas*—*harināma* and the printing press—reverberate loudly across America. May that happen sooner rather than later. Remembering that ISKCON is nondifferent from your body, may we never inflict any wounds on you, and may our love for you be eternally manifested and amplified in our dealings with one another. We ask of you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, all this and more. On this most auspicious day of your appearance, please accept this insignificant gift from us—our lives as an offering unto Your Divine Grace.

Your aspiring servants at New Hastināpura dhāma, Washington, D.C.  
(written by Bhayahari Dāsa)

## Yaśodāpura Ashrama

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

**I offer my respectful obeisances unto His Divine Grace A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who is very dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa on this earth, having taken shelter at His lotus feet.**

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**Our respectful obeisances are unto you, O spiritual master, servant of Sarasvatī Gosvāmī. You are kindly preaching the message of Lord Caitanyadeva and delivering the Western countries, which are filled with impersonalism and voidism.**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you once said:

We have to spend or waste gallons of blood before we can convert a person to Kṛṣṇa consciousness. It is a difficult task. By talking with them, with the rascal and foolish, we tax our brain and spoil our energy, blood.

In this way you sacrificed your life to lead people back to Godhead. And on this most auspicious anniversary of your appearance, the glories of your movement have conquered those difficulties.

People don’t think that a person could live without illicit sex, meat-eating, drinking, and gambling. Therefore they wonder how you inspired thousands of people around the world to come to this standard. The answer is by your purity and spiritual attraction. As you said, “Unless someone is pure, people will not be attracted.” By the sacred power of Vedic education, you attracted thousands of people.

As *jagad-guru* you gave enlightenment. Because you were authorized and without mistake, illusion, cheating, or imperfect senses, your powerful teachings touched the hearts of the people, and thus thousands of fallen souls became attracted. They surrendered to your lotus feet as your *disciples*. “Live like this. You will be happy,” you said to them. And as soon as they followed your instruction, they got the sweetness from the nectar of devotion and became happy.

You brought back the standard of Vedic culture by introducing *saṁskaras* to correct people and bring them to the brahmanical stage. Your strong words were that one should come “from pig stage to brahmanical stage.” Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu also said, *kara* *para*-*upakāra: “Work for the benefit of others.”* Your ISCKON movement has been realizing that order of Śrī Caitanya Mahaprabhu.

And to help people advance you made part of your mission the reestablishment of the *daivī*-*varṇāśrama*-*dharma* system. You said:

At the present moment the whole world is full of rascals. Now if they take to Kṛṣṇa consciousness, select amongst them, just like I am training you are *brāhmaṇa* by training. So one who is prepared to be trained as *brāhmaṇa,* and classify him in the *brāhmaṇa*. One who is trained up as *kṣatriya,* classify him, in this way you take the whole mass of people as *śūdra* and rest, who is neither *brāhmaṇa* nor *kṣatriya* nor *vaiśya,* then he is *śūdra*. That is all, very easy thing. This will be the happiness. When people will be peaceful, happy in their living condition, that will bring happiness. \[Morning Walk, Johannesaburg, 16 October 1975\]

Your purpose in establishing centers in every town and village was to give all living entities a chance to purify themselves, revive their relationship with Kṛṣṇa, and go back home, back to Godhead.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, to serve your movement we have been running a center about a mile from Angkor Wat, the largest Viṣṇu temple in the world. We call this center Yaśodāpura Ashrama. In our Base Camp we offer daily courses on social education that are open to anyone. Khmer and foreigners visitors come, and some of them participate in singing songs, playing music, and learning courses related to traditional art and Vedic culture. Sometimes we conduct *harināma* *saṅkīrtana* or distribute books or *prasādam*. Other activities include teaching English, preaching at nearby Buddhist temples, *pagodas,* or friends’ houses, and publishing your books, along with pamphlets and and our “Angkor Bulletin.” To learn to produce our own food, we maintain a small garden. We are praying that Your Divine Grace and Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu will kindly accept the offerings from our garden.

About thirty miles from our base camp we have been slowly developing a farm community on about thirty acres of land. We pray that in the future it can be one of your *varṇāśrama* community projects. *Jaya* Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Your devotees in Yaśodāpura Ashrama, Cambodia.

## Vyāsa-pūjā Homage from the Publishers

## Bhaktivedanta Book Trust (India)

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you and your instructions are the force that keeps ISKCON together. Your instructions are the safe and sure way for all devotees who want to cross over the ocean of material existence. If we follow your instructions without adding or subtracting anything, then the result is assured.

Our difficulties in following your instructions are due to our past conditioning, and only by your mercy can we hope to be steady.

Your instructions are certainly rooted in the path laid out by the previous *ācāryas,* and we can have full faith in those instructions by being true to the *paramparā*. Your application of the previous *ācāryas’* instructions according to time, place, and circumstance is perfect, a fact proved by the result.

One instruction stands out in my mind more than others, and that is your request to your followers to distribute your books. This one activity, you said, is the way to really please you. So, why is there resistance in our minds to following this important instruction? If we could take to heart this one instruction and carefully implement it, the whole world would change for the better.

This world is becoming more and more intolerable, with pollution and demoniac leaders who aren’t able to help themselves, what to speak of the poor people. Your instructions are like a big sword that can cut through the onslaught of *Māyā*. However, we need the strength to lift that sword, strength that comes through detachment from material desires. Plus, we need to keep the sword sharp by intense devotional practices. In this way we can be successful in our fight with *Māyā*.

It may sound over-simplified, but I truly believe that all we need to do is distribute your books as much as possible, and that Lord *Caitanya* Mahāprabhu will take care of the rest.

Life Is Meant to Please You  
This life is meant to please you;  
If you’re glad, we will be too.  
Your instructions are grease on the wheels,  
But Māyā’s so close on our heels,  
We only guess how the spiritual feels.  
Mahāprabhu’s mercy is great;  
Without it, sealed is our fate.  
Please help us not to take late.  
“Books are the basis,” you said.  
We need to get that in our head  
So Kṛṣṇa nectar can be read.  
Help us get out the lead.  
As you can see, a poet I’m not,  
But it’s try to please you or I rot.  
Such a bracing life is my lot.  
Only your mercy can untie the knot.

Your servants at the Bhaktivedanta Book Trust offices in India.  
(written by Bhīma Dāsa)

## The Bhaktivedanta Book Trust (North America)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace on your appearance day.

In previous ages, there was no need for books. Disciples would hear the Vedic teachings from their gurus and retain them in their well-developed memories. But in the Age of Kali the power of memory has decreased. And therefore Śrīla Vyāsadeva edited the Vedic teachings and set them down in writing. In addition, on the order of his guru Nārada Muni, he composed the *Śrīmad*-*Bhāgavatam,* the spotless *Purāṇa*. In this spotless *Purāṇa*, Kṛṣṇa is present to the people of the Kali-yuga.

The essential Vedic texts, the *Gītā* and the *Bhāgavatam,* were thus set down in written form. The main method of distribution was copying the manuscripts by hand. This method continued through the time of Lord Caitanya. In more recent times, the technology of mechanical book printing spread throughout the world. In India, Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura, one of your predecessors in the line of *Gauḍīya* Vaiṣṇava *ācāryas,* began to use this technology to distribute the classic texts and texts of his own composition. He produced books in the English language, and sent them abroad. His son, your own spiritual master, *Bhaktisiddhānta* Sarasvatī Ṭhākura, expanded the use of modern book production and distribution technologies. It became the central focus of his mission. He called the printing press the Bṛhat Mṛdaṅga. He personally instructed you about the importance of book distribution. He told you that if you ever got any money, you should spend it for printing books. He said that he felt like selling the marble from his main temple and using the money to print books. Over and over he stressed the importance of book production and distribution.

You have fulfilled his desires. You also made book production and distribution the central focus of your International Society for Krishna Consciousness. From the beginning of ISKCON, you engaged your disciples in assisting you in book production and distribution. After a few years, you formally established the Bhaktivedānta Book Trust to perpetually carry on the production and distribution of your books.

By your mercy, we have been engaged in serving you in the BBT you established. On this day, we dedicate ourselves to carrying out this special service to you to the best of our abilities. Praying for your continued mercy, we beg to remain

Your servants at the North American BBT in Los Angeles.  
(written by Drutakarmā Dāsa)

## Bhaktivedanta Book Trust (Northern Europe)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, this past year I have become more seriously aware of how weighty, demanding, and life-absorbing the service you left us is. It requires all our intelligence and all our energy to carry it out. And if we don’t want to be lost from your mission through misunderstanding or sidetracked by personal interest, if we want to help your movement become the spiritually vital organization you envisioned it to be—one that offers your shelter to devotees and your compassion to the world—we must have complete dependence on and unwavering trust in your instructions.

But to develop the humility and simplicity to become a true follower is so difficult. Śrīla Prabhupāda, I miss you deeply and long for you to be here. You wanted us to create, in this day and age—within the movement and within ourselves—“the wonder of an atmosphere surcharged with devotional service to the Lord.” How can we possibly do that without you here, guiding us closely?

Whenever I ask your disciples this they remind me that even when you were here you emphasized the power of your books—that *they* are our association with you, that most devotees had very little opportunity to associate with you when you were physically present, that we are not as bereft as we think. I am never quite emotionally satisfied with this answer, but I know they are right. Your books offer a flood of mercy and unbroken association, free for the taking for me and every other person anywhere in the world, qualified or unqualified, man, woman, or child, and, by the hard work of your followers, in almost any language.

Nārada Muni speaks of his encounter with his own *bhakti-vedāntas* like this:

As they were leaving, those *bhakti-vedāntas,* who are very kind to poor-hearted souls, instructed me in that most confidential subject which is instructed by the Personality of Godhead Himself. By that confidential knowledge, I could understand clearly the influence of the energy of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa, the creator, maintainer, and annihilator of everything. By knowing that, one can return to Him and personally meet Him. \[*Śrīmad-Bhagavatam* 1.5.30–31\]

Like Nārada Muni’s *bhakti-vedāntas,* you were with us so briefly, Śrīla Prabhupāda, yet while you were present you worked so hard to guarantee that you would never be absent from us. In your books, in your association, you made the nature of the transcendentalist attractive and left us with the most confidential knowledge so that we too could return to Kṛṣṇa and personally meet Him. You also ordered us to spare no efforts to give to others what you gave us by making your books available to everyone everywhere. With that order you blessed us with a lifetime of service at your feet.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please allow us the grace to become real disciples. Times have changed since your departure and we have also grown older, and while we may have to adjust how we serve your mission in this way or that, the way forward is still based on the same principles you taught us: full dependence on the holy name and full submission to your mercy. Please help us to live for nothing else but that.

Your servants at the Bhaktivedanta Book Trust offices in Northern European.  
(written by Kaiśorī Dāsī)

## Vyāsa-Pūjā Homages from Other Sources

## ISKCON Congregational Development Ministry

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

ISKCON belongs to you; it’s yours. The ISKCON Congregational Development Ministry is also yours, and we want to exclusively work for you, helping to realign ISKCON’s individual and community dynamics to your vision and values.

The Ministry doesn’t have any administrative power; our only “weapons” are your words and our attempts to represent their spirit in our words, through seminars and publications. The Ministry functions as a cultural operation, working to shape assumptions and perceptions in the field of congregational development, according to what you offered as the blueprint for your ISKCON.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you kindly provided extensive directions in this field, but unfortunately your instructions are sometimes unknown or willfully neglected. Just to give an example: By definition, the “congregation” consists of financially self-sufficient, home-based devotees; therefore charity is a central aspect of their life. “Charity is meant for the householders,” as you state in your purport to *Bhagavad-gītā* 16.1–3. In that same purport—and in many other passages in the *Bhagavad-gītā*, *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* and *Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛta*, as well as in conversations and letters—you kindly provided guidance on how much should be donated and for what purpose: “The householders should earn a livelihood by an honorable means and spend fifty percent of their income to propagate Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world.”

There is no lack of instructions from your side on the “50% principle.” You specifically, consistently, and repeatedly promoted it. Nonetheless, the practice is hardly a common standard in your Society. Some of your followers have started advocating giving ten percent instead, in direct disregard of your words. Of course, they might be motivated by apparently legitimate rationalizations; perhaps they are not clear on the meaning of “income” (before or after expenses?); perhaps they have concluded that 50 percent isn’t a practical guideline; perhaps they are trying to imitate following some of their local religious organizations. For whatever reason, your instructions are eclipsed by other ideas or by materialistic doubts on their efficacy and pertinence.

Actually, when we realize that you were talking about the accumulated money—not one’s full salary—giving fifty percent is revealed as practical and affordable. You wrote in your purport to *Śrīmad-Bhagavatam* 1.17.42: “As we have suggested before, one’s accumulated wealth may be divided into three parts for distribution, namely fifty percent for the service of the Lord, twenty-five percent for the family members, and twenty-five percent for personal necessities.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, your ideas are the best and are most practical. In this as in other areas, your servants at the ISKCON Congregational Development Ministry would like to see your movement enthusiastically realigning with your directives. Today, on the day of your holy appearance in this world, we beg you to kindly improve your servants’ faith in your instructions; to mercifully increase our wisdom and discrimination so that we can apply your vision in externally changing circumstances; and to compassionately bless us with the determination to stick to your teachings, regardless of their circumstantial unpopularity.

ISKCON is yours, and the ISKCON communities that are expanding all over the world should stay firmly at your lotus feet. We are honored and privileged to play a role in this project.

Your servants at the ISKCON Congregational Development Ministry.  
(written by Kaunteya Dāsa)

## ISKCON Daiva-Varrama Ministry

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace. Kindly accept this humble offering from our fledging national ministry promoting the *varṇāśrama* mission.

In this offering we will briefly summarize what is taking place regarding *varṇāśrama* development here in India and, to some extent, around the world. You expressed such a strong desire that your ISKCON society advance the cause of *varṇāśrama* that, although these are but meager developments, I am confident they will be pleasing to hear.

I must first inform you that our ministry held two successful ISKCON LEADS seminars for the North and West Divisions of ISKCON India, both of which were well attended by some forty responsible devotees, representing a total of fifteen temples. We heard comments such as “before the seminar I must admit I was a little sceptical, but after the second day it all became so clear and obvious.” We have heard many similar statements from devotees who initially doubted the import of this crucial element of your preaching mission. These two seminars gave the devotees serving in the ministry additional strength and inspiration to push forward toward achieving the objectives of the *varṇāśrama* movement.

Another important development that took place in both Mumbai and Māyāpur was the formation of the *varṇāśrama* initiative committee. Although we are not allowed to openly call it by that name, but rather “rural development initiative committee” or the “village initiative committee,” those of us involved in setting up this global committee are happy that such a development is taking place. This was made possible by the good will of the members of the Strategic Planning Team, who are working closely with the GBC body in helping to push forward your movement. I believe you will be most happy to hear that one of our identified “smart goals,” which we have started to promote, is producing a well-prepared curriculum for establishing *varṇāśrama* colleges. Yes, finally. In this context, we are very pleased to inform you that some of the Vṛndāvana devotees working at the Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma *gośālā* have agreed to start the Vṛndāvana Varṇāśrama College, which will initially offer courses on making various cow medicines. By October this year we should be coming out with our initial draft of what will soon become a comprehensive curriculum and syllabus booklet to help devotees and communities from any part of the world begin establishing *varṇāśrama* colleges, as you requested us to do so many years ago.

In the latter part of 2010 we established a new national department of the ministry called “Protection, Administration, and Welfare: the Kṣatriya-Dharma.” You so clearly explained that both *brāhmaṇas* and *kṣatriyas* are needed for society to function properly. We will soon be making a presentation about *kṣatriya*-*dharma* at the upcoming meetings of the leadership here in India. We beg your kind blessings for this to move forward.

The Varṇāśrama Initiative Committee is a committee of fifteen devotees from many different countries. We have selected a small executive committee to begin conference calls this month.

Although these small developments are in their initial phases, they have great potential to inform many devotees of the urgency of promoting the *varṇāśrama* mission.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, there are many other important developments taking place, but I cannot share all of these in this brief report. But two small developments worthy of mention are the opening of our first regional office in Śrī-dhāma Māyāpur, in February of this year, and the fourth annual global *varṇāśrama* seminar in Māyāpur, in March. You have many sincere devotees working in different countries to push forward the *varṇāśrama* mission. If I were to list all their names and activities, this offering would become too long.

Other developments include the publication of several new titles: “Ministry Guidelines,” “Make Vṛndāvana Villages” (second edition), “Questions and Answers,” and “Traditional Education—Selected Interviews.” We also have a series of documentaries coming out within a month, covering various aspects of the *varṇāśrama* preaching mission.

I feel encouraged by many recent developments, and I am hoping that you are also happy that these are taking place. We should be doing so much more to please you, and I beg to be forgiven for all my shortcomings in not promoting this aspect of your mission more vigorously. There are many sincere devotees working for the *varṇāśrama* mission, but we need to increase their number many times. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for your constant guidance and encouragement. Kindly shower your mercy so that we can push forward your *varṇāśrama* mission. The world is severely afflicted today and is more and more in need of the simple remedy you have so nicely introduced: the *daiva*-*varṇāśrama* lifestyle.

All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. You remain my constant inspiration and guide.

Your servants at the ISKCON Congregational Development Ministry.  
(written by Bhakti Rāghava Swami)

## ISKCON Ministry for Cow Protection and Agriculture

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Please don’t leave us. We know you would never do that. It would be our hearts broken with pain that would leave you due to bitterness from the many tests of our love for you and Lord Kṛṣṇa. Please stay by our side and never let this happen. Please give us the mercy to see you and the Cowherd Boy in every misfortune.

How does the heart not grow cold after seeing the deaths of so many beloved cows? How does the heart not become bitter after seeing disease ruin the peace of an unpretentious cow? How does the heart not leave the supreme originator of such suffering? Not just once, or twice, but many times after years of caring and having to face the same deterioration and not being able to change the course of the disease because it is beyond changing.

The mind and heart have to change. The miracle is not in the cure, it is in understanding the suffering and how we must embrace it and see Lord Kṛṣṇa in its midst. The suffering will continue as long as there is life. And even though the cow is His most beloved animal, she does not escape old age, disease, and death.

But *Māyā* calls and says, “There is no need for this suffering. You must have done something wrong, you haven’t done enough, and there must be no God because if there were He would not be so cruel as to allow such suffering. Look at the young beautiful calf; look at the strong bull who leads the herd. They should never grow old, they should never die.”

Radhe Shyam died last week. No one really knew how old she was. The vet said she was probably in her mid-twenties. She was a very old cow—in human years one hundred years old or more. She was full of life the day before she died. She led all the old ladies out of the geriatric barn in the first breakout of the season. It was the new, short, fresh grass on Lakshmi’s lawn that attracted her. She was feisty that day. The previous year she had to be fed and watered separately in the pasture, as she had to lay down because she could not keep up with the herd. This happened several times. Then we put her in the geriatric barn in the fall, as she could not keep up at all with the herd. When all the other old ladies came into the barn for the winter, she got quite lively. She surprised us by staying strong during the winter. Then the morning after the breakout, she was lying in the middle barn with Madhava (the ox) standing over her. We went to check on her and she had already left her body. It happened during the night. She was fortunate it happened quickly. It is a far greater test when dying is slow. Hers was a good, long life as a protected cow.

All those great souls that have nursed a dying cow—Śrīla Prabhupāda, we pray that you give them the strength to keep their hearts open to your presence while they try to give their beloved cow comfort. In the most difficult times, you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, are there. In every misfortune you and the Cowherd Boy are there. It is only your mercy that allows us to see you there. Without you, how could anyone’s heart not become cold when the most innocent of creatures suffer?

The deepest of loves develops when the most difficult of times emerge. This is the most sacred joy of a cowherd. In the mud, in the cold, watching the pain, wiping the face, and chanting in the ear of the suffering cow bring Lord Kṛṣṇa to the place when the soul passes. Even though Radhe Shyam did not have our presence at the time of death, she had many years of our association with embraces and chanting of the holy name in her floppy ears.

Bless the cowherds worldwide. Protect them from fatigue and weariness in the face of disease and death. Give them deep love and understanding in such times. Give them joy in times of birthing and youthful calf frolicking. Give them a deep sense of accomplishment and a plentiful harvest from plowing with their oxen. Gift them with rising in the early morning to see their cows basking in the morning sun as the mist leaves the hillsides. Bless them with the peace of the simple life of the humble cowherd unaffected by any desire for adoration, name, or fame. In such a life there is great peace and closeness to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and your most dear cowherd boy, Lord Kṛṣṇa.

Your servants at the International Society for Cow Protection and ISKCON Ministry for Cow Protection and Agriculture.  
(written by Chayā Devī)

## Vaiṣṇavī Ministry

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

It is very hard to decide what to say when you are writing on behalf of half the ISKCON population. I was selected to write this year’s Vyāsa-pūjā offering to Your Divine Grace on behalf of the Vaiṣṇavī Ministry. It happened around six weeks ago in Māyāpur, just before the festival. Mother Mālatī personally invited me to the Vaiṣṇavī Ministry meeting, and she told me to invite whoever I saw. Since this was my first Ministry meeting, I didn’t know what to expect. There were about sixty women packed into a small room in the Conch Guesthouse. After each *mātājī* introduced herself, Mother Mālatī asked several of them to speak about their service to your movement. I was blown away by the dedication of the various *mātājīs* who told about their preaching projects. One is running a project that is attracting new people through yoga classes. Another is successfully training female devotees in a first-class *āśrama* for women. Others are selflessly managing major temples around the world. Some senior women are GBC members.

One thing I liked about the meeting was that the women were very humble. They were dedicating their lives to your mission. I also liked the mood. One lady pointed out that this ministry is about what we as mothers and women can do for you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. The mood was about inspiring each other to serve you to our fullest capacity. It was about being the best we can be, and it had nothing to do with “women’s lib.” You have given us real liberation by giving us knowledge about how to live as devotees of the Lord. Secondly, you have told us how to be chaste women and how to live peacefully in Kṛṣṇa consciousness with husband and children. I should point out that the women taking on the above-mentioned tasks were either young, unmarried women or senior women with grown-up children. As for women in between those two age groups, you always encouraged them to make their priority raising their Kṛṣṇa conscious children. If we make our children our priority and give them our love and a Kṛṣṇa conscious upbringing, down the road we will see the ripened fruits of our endeavors. It is all by your mercy, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Last year I heard Mother Mālatī relate a very special pastime with you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. It was in the early days in Māyāpur, and things were very austere. Mālatī had to cook for you and your godbrothers. It was a great challenge, with many hurdles she had to cross, but she did it. During the meal Mālatī came out to serve something. She was trying her hardest not to be seen, and so she covered her whole head with her sari. But you told your godbrothers, “She would slit her throat for me, and I would do the same for her!”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, on behalf of the Vaiṣṇavī Ministry, I pray that we may all become surrendered servants to you throughout our lives. We each have our particular challenges and services and duties, and we each live in the material world. It is not always easy to be so great and so Kṛṣṇa conscious, but if we have the association of advanced devotees, we can become very strong. Please bless us that we may always progress on the path of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, and that we may give our dependents the same mercy you have shown us. Although you have not been physically present for thirty-four years, it is obvious that you have empowered sincere souls to push on this movement. You reciprocate with whoever sincerely takes up this process of Kṛṣṇa consciousness.Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for taking care of us, and for allowing us to be a part of this*saṅkīrtana* mission.

Your spiritual daughters and granddaughtersat the Vaiṣṇavī Ministry.

## Bhaktivedanta Archives

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

Thank you very much for saving our lives, our souls, and giving us a reason to exist: to help you serve in your mission. Without your divine teachings and continued guidance through the books you so carefully translated and purported, through your recorded lectures and teachings, and through your dedicated followers, we would be lost souls among the other lost souls floundering in the material whirlpool of *saṁsāra*.

Our only purpose in this life is to do whatever we can to serve you—to do what we can to help preserve your legacy of audio recordings, documents, and images for present and future generations of devotees. Thus they may glimpse your transcendental and merciful personality, see a pure devotee, and have faith that you are real and have in fact a valuable gift for us all: true life and true love. This gift is eternal, ever-existent, beyond the constraints of time and space. You have given us the Supreme Godhead—Śrī Kṛṣṇa and Śrīmatī *Rādhārāṇī*, Lord *Nityānanda* and Lord Caitanya.

We are currently fully absorbed in the compelling task of identifying and unraveling a conundrum consisting of multiple bits and pieces of audio recordings on file in the Archives. Steady progress is being made. New audio transcripts are diligently being generated by enthusiastic volunteers and will eventually be added to the VedaBase. Mother Kṣamā is doing an extraordinary service proofreading these, and re-proofreading all the existing transcripts in the current collection. Your many hundreds of lectures and conversations are been carefully secured, and transcripts of these are being arranged in chronological order in the VedaBase. Our ambition is to release an accurate account of the original audio and transcripts in installments; we hope to release the first lot, covering 1977, by the end of this year.

In addition to tasting all the nectar of your recordings and transcripts, we are also immersed in an ocean of visual nectar due to the need to keep up with technology by re-scanning the earliest images of Your Divine Grace, the ever-sweet black-and-white photo collection. The goal is to enter all these images into a comprehensive database so we can tag the images with the information we have documented here at the Bhaktivedanta Archives. By working with your photographer servants, such as Bhārgava Dāsa, Yadubar Dāsa, Viśākhā Devī Dāsī, and Muralīvadana Dāsa, we hope to add as much pertinent information as possible.

Please bestow upon us your benevolent blessings, for without them we feel utterly useless.

Thanking you eternally,  
Your fallen servants at the Bhaktivedanta Archives.

## Bhaktivedanta College (Budapest)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances! All glories to your lotus feet!

On this most auspicious day of your appearance, I would like to tell you about your college here, Bhaktivedanta College. First, the most important event lately was that we could finally move into a permanent building, just beside the Budapest temple. By our merging of the two institutions, the Hare Kṛṣṇa Educational and Cultural Centre was founded, as you may have been informed by Gauramaṇi Prabhu.

Rather than elaborate on the organizational changes, I would like to write about the everyday uplifting experience of seeing you perform a genuine miracle with the students of the College.

As you know, to further the cause of high-level Vaiṣṇava education, we started our second faculty, the training of Vaiṣṇava Yoga Masters. This move has been greatly successful. Public interest in yoga studies is overwhelming, and the people who came to us to study were much more intelligent and pious than the average person, even though most of them were not (yet) *bhaktas* of Kṛṣṇa, and becoming one was not even on their agenda. The certificate they got was in itself worth the study and effort, as yours is the only college in Hungary where they could get it. However, when they joined, they did not yet know you.

The first sign of change was that as they progressed in their studies they somehow developed some strange attachment to your college. They tried not to be absent from the classes. Even those who one might think would never have anything to do with Kṛṣṇa consciousness were strict in their attendance. Their participation in classes and in other functions of the College were a minimiracle we experienced daily. The next sign was that they volunteered to help with various chores: packing when the college moved to its new premises, cleaning, arranging the library, etc. Then they started to appear at temple programs and began to wear Vaiṣṇava attire. Without their noticing it, they were starting to identify themselves as devotees, a fact shown by the questions they asked at classes and by the nature of their problems and the way they shared those with their professors. The transformation could be clearly detected.

But the most moving change was when they began to show the utmost respect toward you and your followers. Even though they sometimes expressed this respect clumsily, their sincerity was so genuine that I was continually wondering whether I was worthy to accept it.

The highest point, however, was the Swan Ball, the introduction of last year’s freshmen, where the program was made up of the students’ productions. I was constantly trying to hold back my tears and could not but think how magnanimous and, at the same time, clever you are, dear *Śrīla Prabhupāda*! For most of the performers were completely unaware that they were expressing their devotion to Lord Kṛṣṇa in various ways, and you, dear *Śrīla Prabhupāda*, are the root and the fosterer of this devotion.

In all sincerity, I can hardly believe my great fortune that I can be an active participant in this miracle. Please never allow me to leave this service of yours, which is sweeter than the sweetest!

Your eternal servants at the Bhaktivedanta College in Budapest, Hungary.  
(written by Mahārāṇī Devī Dāsī)

## Bhaktivedanta College (Radhadesh)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

In your purport to the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 7.6.1 you wrote:

In all the schools, colleges and universities, and at home, all children and youths should be taught to hear about the Supreme Personality of Godhead. In other words, they should be taught to hear the instructions of *Bhagavad-gītā*, to put them into practice in their lives, and thus to become strong in devotional service, free from fear of being degraded to animal life.

Our service at Bhaktivedanta College is a humble attempt to fulfill this instruction, which you gave on various occasions. Charity starts at home. To inspire people in general to teach in their schools, colleges, and universities the principles of pure devotional service explained in the *Bhagavad-gītā* and *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*, it is essential that we ourselves set a proper example by establishing our own successful educational institutions.

We pray for the intelligence and ability to properly execute our service and to overcome whatever challenges we face in fulfilling your desire for systematic Kṛṣṇa conscious education.

Your grateful servants at Bhaktivedanta College, Radhadesh.

## Bhaktivedanta College of Education and Culture

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our prostrated obeisances at the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Loving Grace. All glories to your Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā.

*You books have a touch*stone effect;  
*Studying them allows us with you to connect*;  
*They erase doubts and transform hearts*;  
*Sublime enlightenment they do impart*.  
*Never limited is your compassion*;  
*It extends even to those that are yet unborn*.  
*Your teachings are therefore for all times*.  
*Surrendering to them liberates us from our past crimes*.  
*Your lecture recordings are an unmatched gift to us*;  
*Repeatedly hearing you nurtures our* Kṛṣṇa consciousness.  
*You remain our greatest role model*;  
*Hearing about you helps us remain devotional*.  
*To follow in your footsteps remains our aim*;  
*The glory of any success we can never claim*,  
*For by your mercy only can we accomplish anything*.  
*Our endeavors remain like pearls on your string*.  
On this auspicious day we bow at your feet and pray  
*To dedicate our lives to you in every way*.  
May we be able to introduce more souls to your mission  
*And preach your message with complete dedication*.

Preaching Report: By your causeless mercy, last year 1990 students participated in structured courses based on your sublime books. These courses were conducted in Durban, Guyana, Halifax, Houston, Johannesburg, Mauritius, Māyāpur, Nairobi, Pietermaritzburg, Pretoria, San Diego, Toronto, Trinidad, and Vancouver. In addition to courses on Vaiṣṇava Etiquette, *The Science of Self Realization,* *The Spiritual Master and the Disciple,* *Bhagavad-gita As It Is,* *The Nectar of Instruction,* *The Nectar of Devotion,* *Śrī* *Īśopaniṣad,* and *Śrīmad*-*Bhāgavatam,* the Namāmṛta Program, Level One, was introduced and was well received in several countries. We humbly pray to be of continued service to Your Divine Loving Grace eternally.

Your servants at The Bhaktivedanta College of Education and Culture, Johannesburg, South Africa.  
(written by Mādrī Devī Dāsī)

## Bhaktivedanta Research Centre

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances.

We learn from the Vedic literatures that the Lord creates simply by His breathing and that He impregnates simply by His glance.

It seems to be the same with your words.

Now, perhaps more than ever, we look to your words for guidance on how to continue your mission to deliver mankind from the hopeless struggle for happiness in this material world.

August 15, 1976—Bombay

There was no *darśana* this afternoon, although Śrīla Prabhupāda did go up onto the roof to sit for a while with a few devotees. Then it began to rain, so he returned to his sitting room. There he met with Girirāja and Nitāi prabhus to discuss requirements for a library for the new building. Nitāi has been given Rs. 4,000 by the temple to purchase books for it. Śrīla Prabhupāda said the library should contain all his own books, those of all the *ācāryas,* as well as the *Upaniṣads* and other bona fide Vedic texts. \[Hari Śauri Prabhu’s *Transcendental Diary*\]

Thus in 1976 you instructed that a library be formed to house all the works of the *ācāryas*. You had already described the importance of such a project in a letter to Acyutānanda Swami in 1972:

Treat this matter very seriously and thoroughly, and take all precautions to protect this wonderful boon of literatures forever. Yadubara may photograph every page, never mind Bengali or English or any other language, and later we shall see where to send the copies to different places. You also write to Yadubara at Bombay in this connection and request him to join you in Birnagar immediately. I have also written him. These items are very, very priceless and are a great treasure house of Vaisnava lore, so be very careful in the matter and take all precautions to guard them.

Now at last we have taken up this project. We started in 2009 and now have a library to house the works of our *ācāryas* in the original languages. We have begun the search for original manuscripts, which is bearing fruit. We are even discovering works that we had no idea existed.

The library is situated in the place where you took your birth in this world: Kolkata. Here we are storing this “great treasure house of Vaisnava lore” so that present and future generations can peruse the original writings, often in the original handwriting, which comprise the reference works of our priceless heritage. If we cannot secure the original works, we are taking digital photographs of the works and will soon offer them online to scholars and devotees.

In another letter to Acyutānanda Swami in 1972, you made reference to such a project:

Regarding the manuscripts, you may photograph all of the pages, then we shall type them later. If there is shortage of film, I have instructed Karandhara to send you a large supply of the appropriate films for photographing the pages. Or you may purchase there and we shall pay from Book Fund. These are important works and they must be preserved forever, so what is that expense?

From your words Śrīla Prabhupāda, we can see that such a library was certainly a project you wanted. We can also see that you considered it of great importance to your developing movement, ISKCON.

We humbly offer this library to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. We are working to fulfill your desires, and as always your desires are for the benefit of mankind, not only in India but throughout the world.

We offer our most humble obeisances to your words and your desires, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and consider ourselves most fortunate to have the opportunity to serve them.

Your humble servants at the Bhaktivedanta Research Centre, Kolkata, India.

## Festival of India

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*In Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (3.25.8) Devahūti says to Lord Kapila, “Your Lordship is my only means of getting out of this darkest region of ignorance because You are my transcendental eye, which, by Your mercy only, I have attained after many, many births.” In your purport to this verse you write, “. . . if one is fortunate enough to get the association of a spiritual master like Kapila Muni or His representative, then by his grace one can be delivered from the mire of ignorance.”

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

You have given yourself to everyone on this planet. Simply by reading your books, anyone can have your association. As long as your transcendental books are available, conditioned souls are going to be delivered from the mire of ignorance.

With this purpose in mind, the Festival of India travels from city to city to facilitate Kṛṣṇa conscious events.
